final design will be Selection of a pavement section is typically a choice relative to a higher initial

cost and lower long-term maintenance, or a lower initial cost with more frequent maintenance.

For this reason, we recommend that the Owner participate in the selection of the proposed

pavement sections planned for the site. Site grading plans should include provisions for sloping

of the subgrade soils in proposed pavement areas, so that passive drainage of the pavement

section(s) can proceed uninterrupted during the life of the project. The proposed pavement areas

should be prepared as indicated in the Site Preparation and Earthwork section of this report. As

noted above, the uncontrolled structural fill soils may remain below pavement sections if

observed to be firm and unyielding.

Based on discussions with the project Civil Engineer and owner, it is our understanding that the

new pavement sections will be constructed in 2 phases. During early stages of project

construction, the subgrade along new roadway alignments will be stripped of unsuitable soils and

the gravel base layer, as described below, will be placed. The final lift of Crushed Surfacing Top

Course and asphalt or concrete pavement will be placed during the final stages of project

construction. To provide additional support during construction activities we recommend the

placement of a woven geotextile such as Tencate® Mirafi® RS280i (or performance equivalent)

at the interface of structural fill and native alluvial soils for all pavement sections.

Flexible Pavement

Based on correspondence with the project team we understand that a single asphalt pavement

section will be utilized for the planned development. We recommend pavement sections consist

of 3 inches of Class ½-inch HMA asphalt above 2 inches of Crushed Surfacing Top Course (CSTC)

meeting criteria set forth in the Washington State Department of Transportation (WSDOT)

Standard Specification 9-03.9(3). The base material for

16 inches of gravel borrow (with 100% passing the 2-inch sieve) or

Crushed Surfacing Base Bourse (CSBC) as classified by WSDOT 9-03.9(3) Standards and

Specifications. This proposed section will provide appropriate support for firelanes, parking and

any other areas that will be accessed by heavy traffic or higher volumes, such as moving trucks

or delivery lanes.

Concrete Pavement Sections

Concrete pavements could be used for access drives, parking areas, sidewalks, aprons, and

support structures. Design of concrete pavements is a function of concrete strength,

reinforcement steel, and the anticipated loading conditions for the drive lanes. GeoTest expects

that concrete pavement sections, if utilized, will be at least 6 inches thick and be founded on a

minimum of 8 inches of compacted gravel base. For design purposes, a vertical modulus of

subgrade reaction of 200 pounds per cubic inch (pci) should be expected for concrete elements

constructed over properly placed and compacted Import structural fill. The design of concrete

access and parking areas will need to be performed by a structural engineer. GeoTest

recommends that subgrade soils supporting concrete pavement sections include minor grade

changes to allow for passive drainage away from the pavement.

Stormwater Infiltration Feasibility

It is our opinion that portions of the native alluvium underlying the site within the vicinity of TP

4, TP-5, TP-8, TP-9, and TP-11 may be suitable for onsite infiltration. A pervasive groundwater

table was encountered at TP-10 and HA/MW-4, located in the northwest corner of the site, at

depths of 8.5 and 6 feet BGS during our initial explorations in November of 2023. Additionally,

GeoTest previously performed a wet season groundwater monitoring study which determined

groundwater was not observed within 9 feet of the surface during 2023-2024 wet season

monitoring at the locations of MW-1, MW-2, and MW-5 while groundwater was recorded at 7.06

feet BGS at the location of MW-3 and above ground surface at MW-4. Per Site Suitability Criteria

(SSC), Volume 5, Section 5, of the Manual, Depth to Bedrock, Water Table or Impermeable Layer

,

proposed stormwater facilities require 5 feet of vertical separation between the seasonal

groundwater high and the bottom of an infiltration facility if the design does not account for

mounding. If a mounding analysis is performed, the vertical separation may be reduced to 3 feet,

per the Manual.

Provided adequate separation can be maintained from high groundwater elevations, we

recommend PIT testing be performed to determine infiltration rates once the location and

elevation of stormwater infiltration facilities have been determined.

Stormwater Treatment

The stormwater facilities on-site may require some form of pollutant pretreatment with an

amended soil prior to on-site infiltration or offsite discharge. The reuse of on-site topsoil is often

the most sustainable and cost-effective method for pollutant treatment purposes. Cation

exchange capacities, organic contents, and pH of site subsurface soils were also tested to

determine possible pollutant treatment suitability.

Cation exchange capacity, organic content, and pH tests were performed by Northwest

Agricultural Consultants on three soil samples collected from the test pit explorations. A

summary of the laboratory test results is presented in Table 3 below.

Suitability for onsite pollutant treatment is determined in accordance with 2019 Stormwater

Management Manual for Western Washington (SMMWW). Soils with an organic content of

greater than or equal to 1 percent and a cation exchange capacity of greater than or equal to 5

meq/100 grams are characterized as suitable for stormwater treatment. Topsoil, such as that

encountered on the site, typically meets suitability criteria. Based on the results shown in Table

2, the near-surface alluvium in the upper 4 feet appears to be suitable for stormwater treatment.

However, the elevated fines of the alluvium may result in low infiltration rates that could affect

the design and feasibility of treatment facilities.

Geotechnical Consultation and Construction Monitoring

GeoTest recommends that we be involved in the project design review process. The purpose of

the review is to verify that the recommendations presented in this report are understood and

incorporated in the design and specifications.

We also recommend that geotechnical construction monitoring services be provided. These

services should include observation by GeoTest personnel during structural fill placement,

compaction activities and subgrade preparation operations to confirm that design subgrade

conditions are obtained beneath the areas of improvement.

Periodic field density testing should be performed to verify that the appropriate degree of

compaction is obtained. The purpose of these services is to observe compliance with the design

concepts, specifications, and recommendations of this report. If subsurface conditions differ

from those anticipated before the start of construction, GeoTest would be pleased to provide

revised recommendations appropriate to the conditions revealed during construction.

GeoTest is available to provide a full range of materials testing and special inspection during

construction as required by the local building department and the International Building Code.

This may include specific construction inspections on materials such as reinforced concrete,

reinforced masonry, wood framing, and structural steel. These services are supported by our fully

accredited materials testing laboratories.

Our site explorations indicate subsurface conditions at the dates and locations indicated. It is not

The analyses, conclusions, and recommendations contained in this report are based on site

conditions to the limited depth and time of our explorations, a geological reconnaissance of the

area, and a review of previously published USGS geological information for the site. If variations

in subsurface conditions are encountered during construction that differs from those contained

within this report, GeoTest should be allowed to review the recommendations and, if necessary,

make revisions. If there is a substantial lapse of time between submission of this report and the

EXHIBIT 13

December 1, 2025 Received

12/19/2025

Stephanie Rude

Ravnik & Associates

Transmitted via email

RE: Water Availability

P26227, P26234, P130319, & P118175

Commercial, Multi-family Residential

Dear Ravnik & Associates:

In response to your request, Skagit Public Utility District has reviewed the above location and proposed

project for water availability and offers the following comments:

 The PUD presently owns and operates a 12-inch diameter water pipeline on Market

Street and an 8-inch pipeline is stubbed to the northeast corner of P130319.

 Water service will only be provided if waterlines with sufficient supply are available and

the site’s location where service is to be installed is contiguous to a water main, unless otherwise

allowed within the PUD Water Policy Manual.

 The PUD has sufficient supply to furnish domestic water to each property. The PUD’s

ability to supply water in excess of domestic use for fire flow demand is dependent on a hydraulic

analysis of the water system related to your proposed project, as well as the extent of the project’s

anticipated needs.

 Upon receipt of the necessary application information, fees, site and billing address, and

parcel number, a metered water service for each p property can be obtained in accordance with the

PUD’s Water Policy Manual. PUD fees can be fofound in Appendix A of the Water Policy Manual,

which can be viewed at the PUD office or online at www.skagitpud.org/customers/water-policy

manual.

 Estimated static pressure in the existing 12-inch waterline is 79 pounds per square inch.

 The proposed project will result in specific backflow protection requirements.

The comments in this letter are based on information available at the time of writing. Modification to the

water system or policy changes can make the information provided herein outdated. A re-evaluation of

the comments is necessary one year after the date of this letter.

Sincerely,

Courtney Shilling

Engineering Technician

Received

6/1/2026Chicago Title

Preliminary Wetland Mitigation Plan prepared by Green Earth

PROJECT SITE AND DESCRIPTION

The project site, hereafter referred to as the "Site," is situated at 2198, 2166, 2164, and 2134 Market

Street, within NE ¼ Section 18, Township 34 North, Range 4 East W.M (Enclosure, Sheet 1). It

encompasses an 8.56-acre site comprised of four parcels, Parcel #’s P26227, P26234, P130319, and

P118175.

The proposed development includes four flex-use and storage buildings on the western half of the Site,

totaling approximately 94,580 SF of building footprint. Construction will occur in two phases: Phase 1

will likely include the northern two buildings, surfacing, and utilities (anticipated start fall 2025),

followed by Phase 2 with the remaining two buildings approximately five years later. Phase 3 proposes

a four-story, mixed-use building on the eastern half of the property with a footprint of 31,992 SF.

Commercial, retail, or service uses will be incorporated on the first floor as required by the City, but the

applicant intends the remainder of the building to be multi-family residential, preliminarily planned at

approximately 128 apartment units. Construction sequencing will progress with installation of erosion

control and BMPs, followed by rough grading and utility installation. A detailed sequence is provided in

Appendix B.

Stormwater treatment, detention, and flow control for new and replaced hard surfaces will be provided

in accordance with the City of Mount Vernon Municipal Code and the 2019 Department of Ecology

Stormwater Management Manual, meeting Minimum Requirements #1–9. Stormwater runoff from

pollution-generating impervious surfaces will be collected, treated through BioPod Biofilter

Underground Vaults providing enhanced water quality treatment, and discharged to underground

infiltration trenches located beneath asphalt parking and drive lanes. These infiltration facilities are

designed to detain and infiltrate stormwater into the underlying soils while providing flow control

consistent with DOE standards, thereby reducing peak discharge rates and protecting downstream

conveyance systems.

Three of the six infiltration systems include an emergency overflow control structure that allows

controlled release to the City-owned storm system within Market Street during rare, high-intensity storm

events. The infiltration design achieves over 98% onsite infiltration of developed runoff and passes the

Low Impact Development (LID) performance standard. No direct discharge to adjacent wetlands is

proposed, and the system is designed to maintain existing hydrologic conditions and prevent alteration

to nearby wetland hydroperiods.

Proposed Impacts

This planned apartment complex has been designed to maximize available developable space, utilize

Best Management Practices (BMPs), and to mitigate unavoidable adverse impacts to the functions and

values of impacted critical areas (Enclosure, Sheet 3). Wetland A is a freshwater emergent (PEM),

Category III wetland, and is subject to development restrictions and critical areas protections under state

and local regulatory authority.

The unavoidable direct and indirect impacts are summarized below (Table 3) and in the following

sections. A total of 14,939 square feet of unavoidable direct wetland impacts will occur as a result of the

proposed commercial facilities and associated infrastructure.

Compensatory Mitigation

On-site mitigation opportunities were considered; however, due to development and habitat

fragmentation in the immediate vicinity, due to the feasibility of the applicant’s cost-benefit analysis,

and because even greater functional lift opportunities exist at a mitigation bank downstream of the site

and in close proximity, it was deemed a greater overall benefit to society to proceed to compensating

for this project’s proposed impacts at the nearby mitigation bank According to SCC 14.24.230(3), "when

credits from a certified wetland mitigation bank are used, the replacement ratios shall be consistent with

the requirements of the bank’s certification." Therefore, in lieu of the standard Skagit County mitigation

4

ratios, the certified mitigation bank's impact-to-credit purchase ratios are applied to this proposal (see

below).

Impacted Wetland Functions

Water Quality Function Impact

Wetland A plays a moderate role in water quality improvement, with a water quality function score of 7

(GEO 2025). The wetland is situated in a developed area and is influenced by stormwater runoff from

adjacent commercial properties and I-5. The wetland's depressional nature allows it to temporarily

retain water, facilitating sediment trapping and some nutrient uptake. However, its effectiveness in

filtering pollutants is limited due to its fragmented condition and previous disturbances, including

agricultural activities. The dominance of reed canary grass (Phalaris arundinacea), an invasive species,

also reduces its overall filtration capacity by outcompeting more effective native wetland vegetation.

Despite these limitations, Wetland A still provides some water quality benefits by retaining surface

water.

Hydrology Function Impact

Wetland A functions as a depressional wetland with seasonal inundation, receiving a hydrologic function

score of 7 (GEO 2025). It plays a limited yet valuable role in stormwater retention and attenuation,

helping regulate local surface water levels. The wetland primarily relies on direct precipitation and

minimal runoff from the adjacent I-5 and commercial property for its hydrologic inputs. Urban

infrastructure has significantly altered its natural hydrology, with storm drain systems and private

development reducing flow and recharge potential. The presence of Mt. Vernon very fine sandy loam, a

well-drained, typically upland soil, facilitates subsurface water infiltration and supports long-term

hydrologic function. The wetland remains an active component of the local hydrologic network, helping

to mitigate peak flows and temporarily store water during heavy rainfall events.

Habitat Function Impact

Wetland A has a relatively low habitat function score of 3 due to its fragmented condition and extensive

surrounding high-intensity development (GEO 2025). Although classified as a Category III wetland, it

supports only reed canary grass, an invasive plant species, which in turn influences habitat availability

for wildlife, and reduces biodiversity and habitat complexity. The immediate surrounding uplands

contain herbaceous species well adapted to disturbed areas such as common tansy (Tanacetum vulgare)

and Poa  sp. The wetland’s small size and isolation further diminish its habitat value, as it lacks

connectivity to larger, more diverse wetland systems. While the wetland still provides some ecological

benefits, its ability to support a wide range of species is constrained by past disturbances and ongoing

development pressures in the surrounding area.

SUBJECT: Save on Storage Transportation Concurrency Review

MV #ENGR25-0286 / TSI 225007:2511

INTRODUCTION

This memorandum documents the transportation concurrency review for the Save on Storage

development (the Project) proposed west of Market Street and east of Pacific Place in Mount Vernon.

PROJECT DESCRIPTION

The Project siteincluding Skagit County tax parcels P26227, P26234, P130319 and P118175, comprises

,

8.6 acres and is zoned C-2 CommercialThe site is bordered by commercial businesses to the north

. ,

south, and eastand I-5 to the westA vicinity map is shown in Figure 1.

, .

Figure 1. Vicinity Map

The site is currently vacant and undeveloped. There is no existing use on the site. The Project proposes a

new development of five total buildings within the four adjoining parcels. The Project proposes four

commercial buildings on the western half of the site, supporting personal services, automobile repairs,

furniture repairs, and other similar business operations consistent with ITE Land Use Code 110 General

Light Industrial. On the eastern side, the Project proposes a 4-story U-shaped hotel building with 240 hotel

16932 Woodinville-Redmond Road | Suite A206 | Woodinville, WA 98072 | 425-883-4134

Save on Storage (ENGR25-0286)

February 20, 2026

room units (60 per floor). Three driveway accesses are proposed. An existing driveway approximately 190

feet north of Pacific Place will be widened, a new main driveway entrance will align with Pacific Place, and

a new third driveway entrance will be located approximately 265 feet south of Pacific Place.

EXISTING ROADWAYS

Market Street

Market Street is a north-south urban collector located to the east of the Project. Market Street begins

approximately 450 feet north of the project site where it intersects with E Stewart Rd and continues along

the east side of the Project site where it forms a T-intersection with Pacific Place. Market Street

continues south, curving southwest at the Project’s SE corner.

In the Project vicinity, Market Street includes two-travel lanes with a parking lane on the east side and no

parking barriers and signs along the west side (Project side). The street includes curb, gutter, and five

foot sidewalk on the east side onlyThe speed limit is 25 mph.

.

Pacific Place

Pacific Place is a two-lane east-west urban collector east of the Project. The street begins at the T

intersection with Market Street at the Project site and intersects with Riverside Dr 500 feet to the east. At

the project frontage, the street includes two travel lanes with two parking lanes with marked parking

stalls. Curb, gutter, and six-foot sidewalks are present on both sides of the street from the Project

frontage to the intersection with Riverside Dr. The speed limit is 25 mph. A map of the arterial street

system in the Project vicinity is shown in Figure 2.

TRANSPORTATION CONCURRENCY STANDARDS

This transportation concurrency review is based on Mount Vernon Municipal Code (MVMC) concurrency

and Level of Service (LOS) standards, including:

16932 Woodinville-Redmond Road | Suite A206 | Woodinville, WA 98072 | 425-883-4134

Save on Storage (ENGR25-0286)

Transportation Concurrency Review

February 20, 2026

Page 5 of 8

• Level of service standards stated in MVMC 14.10.080

• Modified LOS standards stated in MVMC 14.10.060

• Concurrency requirements for “Category D: More Than 75 Peak Hour Trips” stated in MVMC

14.10.090

1. Street Frontage. Three-quarter street LOS improvements must be in Place on the project street

frontage.

2. Adjacent Street System. Three-quarter street LOS improvements must be in Place on the adjacent

street system to the point where they connect to an arterial street that meets the three-quarter street LOS

on the same side of the street as the development.

3. Capacity LOS. Intersections and segments impacted by traffic from the development as identified in

the project traffic impact analysis shall be evaluated for traffic capacity LOS and street design standards

and requirements. Intersections and segments on the arterial street system that are impacted by peak

hour traffic generated by the development shall be required to meet capacity LOS standards and street

design standards. All or a portion of the development shall be denied or delayed until deficient

intersections meet traffic capacity LOS standards and/or street design standards.

D. More Than 75 Peak Hour Trips. If a project or any phase of a project generates more than 75 peak hour

trips, concurrency impacts and mitigation will be determined by the city council based on

recommendations by the Planning commission and/or hearing examiner following a recommendation by

the city engineer. If the underlying permit provides for an appeal to the city council, the city council will

determine the concurrency impacts and mitigation at a single consolidated open record hearing on any

appeal of the underlying permit approval and/or SEPA determination. (Ord. 3236 § 1, 2005; Ord. 2950,

1999; Ord. 2839 § 9, 1997).

TRAFFIC IMPACT ANALYSIS

MVMC 14.10.100 requires the following elements to be addressed in the Traffic Impact Analysis.

Pedestrian Safety LOS

The site frontage on Market Place lacks adequate pedestrian facilities.

Frontage improvements including on-street parking, curb, gutter and sidewalk are necessary on the west

side of street to meet Pedestrian Safety LOS.

The intersection of S 1st St/Freeway Dr & W Division Street operates at LOS F before and after the addition

at this location as Project #15.

experience LOS degradation with the addition of Project trips. Intersection capacity improvements are

identified in the Transportation Element of the Mount Vernon Comprehensive Plan for the deficient

intersection, and no Project mitigation is required.

On-Site LOS

On-site parking and vehicle and pedestrian circulation are adequate for the proposed site uses.

Street Design Standard LOS

Market Street is an urban collector and is fully improved north of the site including one lane of travel in

each direction, and on-street parking, curb, gutter and sidewalk on both sides of the street. The site

frontage includes one lane of travel in each direction, a paved shoulder on the west side and parking,

curb, gutter and sidewalk on the east side of street.

Frontage improvements including, curb, gutter and sidewalk are necessary on the west side of street to

’

meet Design Standard LOS. The improvements should be consistent with project T-25-06 in the Citys

2026 – 2031 Transportation Improvement Plan

Transit LOS

Skagit Transit routes 207 and 208 are within walking distance of the site.

Transit is expected to be minimally impacted by the Project. Transit LOS is satisfied.

Nonmotorized Transportation LOS

Market Place is a planned bicycle route in the Mount Vernon Comprehensive PlanThe existing street

.

section lacks bicycle lanes and sidewalks on the west side

.

Curb and gutter and bicycle lane and sidewalk are necessary on the west side of street to meet

Nonmotorized Transportation LOS.

Pavement Condition LOS

The existing pavement on Market Place north of Pacific Place appears to have been reconstructed along

the site frontage based on Google Streetview images from September 2024. Pavement condition south of

Pacific Place is fair to poor exhibiting continuous transverse and longitudinal cracking.

It is recommended that existing pavement west of the street centerline south of Pacific Place be

reconstructed along the site frontage to meet Pavement Condition LOS.

TRANSPORTATION IMPACT FEES

Transportation impact fees are adjusted annually by the City of Mount Vernon to account for inflation. As

of January 2025, the transportation impact fee rate for non-residential groups is $1,334 per PM peak hour

trip. The Project’s preliminary transportation impact fee is calculated below.

[205 PM peak hour trips] x [$1334/PM peak hour trip] = $273,470

FINDINGS

Findings of this transportation concurrency review are summarized below.

1. The Project will generate 205 new PM peak hour trips.

2. Project-generated trips will result in no new traffic capacity LOS deficiencies.

3. Segment 4009 Hoag Rd is impacted by 35 PM peak Hour trips and is an existing segment LOS

failure. An alternate route for these trips is available.

4. Mitigation is required to meet Design Standard, Pedestrian Safety, Nonmotorized, and Pavement

Condition LOS.

RECOMMENDATIONS

The following recommended conditions for approval are provided for consideration. Final determination

of mitigation requirements will be at the discretion of the City Engineer and City Council.

1. Frontage improvements including curb and gutter and bicycle lane sidewalk are necessary on the

west side of street to meet Street Design Standard Pedestrian Safety, and Nonmotorized LOS.

The frontage improvements shall be consistent with the future on-street bike lanes proposed for

Market St. in TIP Project T-25-06

2. The existing pavement west of the Market Place centerline south of Pacific Place should be

reconstructed along the site frontage to meet Pavement Condition LOS.

3. Any new or modified pedestrian facilities, including sidewalks, driveway cuts, curb ramps, and on

site facilities, must satisfy current Americans with Disabilities Act (ADA) standards. An ADA

compliant pedestrian access route shall be provided from the site to the east side of Market

Street at Pacific Pl including ADA compliant curb ramps.

4. The Project’s preliminary transportation impact fee is $273,470.

5. The city should program non-motorized improvements for Segment 4009 Hoag Rd in the near

future.

SEPA ENVIRONMENTAL REVIEW & ISSUANCE OF A MITIGATED DETERMINATION OF NON-SIGNIFICANCE

(MDNS): The City has reviewed the proposed project for probable adverse environmental impacts and expects

to issue a Mitigated Determination of Non-significance (MDNS). This determination is based on the following

findings and conclusions:

• The City (who is the lead agency) has determined that the requirements for environmental analysis,

protection, and mitigation measures have been adequately addressed with the City’s existing

development regulations and comprehensive plan adopted under chapter 36.70A RCW, and in other

applicable local, state, or federal laws or rules, as provided by RCW 43.21C.240 and WAC 197-11-158.

Our agency will not require any additional mitigation measures under SEPA.

As permitted under the WAC 197-11-350 and 355, the City of Mount Vernon is using the Optional DNS

process to give notice that an MDNS is likely to be issued. Comment periods for the project and the proposed

MDNS are integrated into a single comment period. There will be no comment period following the issuance

of the Threshold Mitigated Determination of Non-Significance (MDNS). A 10-day appeal period will follow the

issuance of this MDNS. THIS MAY BE YOUR ONLY OPPORTUNITY TO COMMENT ON THE ENVIRONMENTAL

IMPACTS OF THE PROPOSED PROJECT.

Agencies, tribes, and the public are encouraged to review and comment on the proposed project and its

probable environmental impacts. Comments must be submitted by the date noted in this document to the

above-identified Lead Agency Contact.

PROJECT DESCRIPTION: The Project proposes a new development of five commercial buildings on the west

half of the site totaling approximately 96390 square feet, supporting mini-storagepersonal services,

,,

automobile repairs, furniture repairs, and other similar business operations. On the east side of the site, the

Project proposes a 4-story U-shaped hotel building with 210 hotel room units. Site development also includes

associated parking (262 surface parking stalls), landscaping, trash enclosures, and other accessory

improvements.

The site contains a Category III wetland located in the northwest corner of the site. The wetland is proposed

to be filled, and mitigated with purchase of credits from an existing wetland bank in Skagit County. The site is

also considered an erosion hazard area, due to soils present, as well as a seismic hazard area, due to the high

potential for liquefaction. The site is also located within FEMA’s A21 Zone.

On-site stormwater runoff will be directed to six onsite infiltration facilities, placed below the proposed

asphalt parking and drivelanes which will detain and infiltrate runoff. Runoff from pollution generating

surfaces will be directed toward three underground stormwater treatment vaults prior to entering the

infiltration trenches. From these three infiltration systems, stormwater will have a controlled release from the

SEPA MDNS | NOTICE OF APPLICATION Page 1 of 4

Save on Storage, Critical Areas and SEPA, PLAN25-0449 and ENGR25-0286

onsite drainage system into the City-owned storm system located on Market Street, via an outlet control

structure. The other three infiltration trenches are designed to infiltrate all runoff.

Approximately 2,209 linear feet (lf) of 2- and 4-inch potable water line, 2,785 lf of 6- and 8-inch sanitary sewer

lines and structures, and 3,481 linear ft of 6-, 8-, and 12-inch storm sewer conveyance lines and dry utilities

(power, cable, fiber, etc) will be constructed/installed to serve the proposed development.

Material will be exported and imported for site development. Specifically, an estimated 16,775 cubic yards

(cy) of material will be exported, and an estimated 77,675 cy of material will be imported.

CONDITIONS BEING CONSIDERED TO MITIGATE ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACTS:

1. All construction personnel associated with this project shall receive unanticipated discovery protocol

training (UDP) prior to any ground-disturbing activities occurring. This required training shall be

completed by a professional archaeologist. The Applicant shall submit confirmation to the City with

the dates and names of all construction personnel having received this training.

a. A copy of the Unanticipated Discoveries Protocol (UDP) shall be on the project site at all

times.

b. In the event that any ground-disturbing (or other) activities uncover protected cultural

material (e.g., bones, shell, stone or antler tools) or human remains all work in the immediate

vicinity shall stop, the area shall be secured, and any equipment moved to a safe distance

away from the location. The on-site superintendent shall then follow the steps specified in the

UDP.

2. Biological Opinion Required. The applicant shall submit a completed Biological Opinion Checklist to

the satisfaction of the Development Services Department documenting that the project does not have

an adverse effect on endangered species or their critical habitat prior to any permits authorizing land

disturbing activities being issued.

MITIGATED DETERMINATION OF NON-SIGNIFICANCE (MDNS)

DATE OF ISSUANCE: 08/04/2026

APPLICATION NAME & FILE #: Save on Storage, Critical Areas and SEPA, PLAN25-0449 and ENGR25-0286

DESCRIPTION OF PROPOSAL: The development of four commercial buildings having a combined footprint area of

approximately 96,390 square feet (sf) on the west half of the site with proposed uses supporting mini-storage, personal

services, automobile repairs, furniture repairs, and other similar business operations. On the east side of the site a four

story u-shaped hotel building with a footprint area of approximately 31,992 sf on each floor with a total of 210 hotel

rooms. Site development also includes parking (262 surface parking stalls), landscaping, trash enclosures, and other

accessory improvements.

The site contains a Category III wetland located in the northwest corner of the site. The wetland is proposed to be filled,

and mitigated with purchase of credits from an existing wetland bank in Skagit County. The site is also considered an

erosion hazard area, due to soils present, as well as a seismic hazard area, due to the high potential for liquefaction. The

site is also located within FEMA’s A21 Zone.

On-site stormwater runoff will be directed to six onsite infiltration facilities, placed below the proposed asphalt parking

and drivelanes which will detain and infiltrate runoff from non-pollution-generating impervious surfaces. Runoff from

pollution generating surfaces will be directed toward three underground stormwater treatment vaults prior to entering

the infiltration trenches. From these three infiltration systems, stormwater will have a controlled release from the onsite

drainage system into the City-owned storm system located on Market Street, via an outlet control structure. The other

three infiltration trenches are designed to infiltrate all runoff.

Approximately 2,209 linear feet (lf) of 2- and 4-inch potable water line, 1,610 lf of 8- and 12-inch waterline for fire

protection, 2,785 lf of 6- and 8-inch sanitary sewer lines and structures, and 3,481 linear ft of 6-, 8-, and 12-inch storm

sewer conveyance lines and dry utilities (power, cable, fiber, etc) will be constructed/installed to serve the proposed

development.

Material will be exported and imported for site development. Specifically, an estimated 16,775 cubic yards (cy) of

material will be exported, and an estimated 77,675 cy of material will be imported.

PROJECT LOCATION: The property is described by the Skagit County Assessor as parcels P26227, P118175, P130319 and

P26234, is located on the west side of Market Street and east side of Interstate 5, at the intersection of Market Street

and Pacific Place, is within a portion of the NE ¼ of Section 18, Township 34N, Range 04E, W.M., and is at latitude

THE MITIGATION MEASURES DEEMED NECESSARY FOR THIS PROJECT ARE LISTED BELOW:

1. All construction personnel associated with this project shall receive unanticipated discovery protocol training

(UDP) prior to any ground-disturbing activities occurring. This required training shall be completed by a

professional archaeologist. The Applicant shall submit confirmation to the City with the dates and names of all

construction personnel having received this training.

a. A copy of the Unanticipated Discoveries Protocol (UDP) shall be on the project site at all times.

b. In the event that any ground-disturbing (or other) activities uncover protected cultural material (e.g.,

bones, shell, stone or antler tools) or human remains all work in the immediate vicinity shall stop, the

area shall be secured, and any equipment moved to a safe distance away from the location. The on-site

superintendent shall then follow the steps specified in the UDP.

2. Biological Opinion Required. The applicant shall submit a completed Biological Opinion Checklist to the

satisfaction of the Development Services Department documenting that the project does not have an adverse

effect on endangered species or their critical habitat prior to any permits authorizing land disturbing activities

being issued.

E. ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACTS AND MITIGATION

In compliance with RCW 43.21C. 240, project environmental review addresses only those project impacts that

are not adequately addressed under existing development standards and environmental regulations.  The

elements of the environment and the project related impacts, mitigation measures and nexus information are

provided to document a portion of the environmental analysis completed in support of the issuance of this SEPA

determination for the below-listed elements where there are multiple regulations different agencies administer.

1. EARTH

Impacts:  An estimated 16,775 cubic yards (cy) of material will be exported, and an estimated 77,675 cy

of material will be imported.

Mitigation Measures:  Listed within the applicant’s environmental checklist as well as required

compliance with the City’s existing standards and regulations for land disturbing. Construction best

management practices will be utilized to minimize potential impacts from earthwork grading and

clearing on the site.

The applicant’s contractor(s) will be required to comply with the City’s code requirements related to

stormwater runoff and site grading. The applicant will also be required to obtain and comply with the

WA State Department of Ecology’s NPDES permit and their associated requirements.

Nexus:  MVMC Chapters 13.33 (Storm Water Drainage Utility), 15.04 (Building Code), 15.16 (Grading,

Excavation and Fill), 15.18 (Land Clearing), and Mount Vernon Engineering Standards.

2. STORMWATER

Impacts:  On-site stormwater runoff will be directed to six onsite infiltration facilities, placed below the

proposed asphalt parking and drivelanes which will detain and infiltrate runoff from non-pollution

generating impervious surfaces. Runoff from pollution generating surfaces will be directed toward three

underground stormwater treatment vaults prior to entering the infiltration trenches. From these three

infiltration systems, stormwater will have a controlled release from the onsite drainage system into the

City-owned storm system located on Market Street, via an outlet control structure. The other three

infiltration trenches are designed to infiltrate all runoff.

Mitigation Measures: Listed within the applicant’s environmental checklist, detailed within their

Stormwater Analysis prepared by a licensed Professional Engineer, shown on their construction plans,

and the mitigation measures outlined with this MDNS. In addition, the applicant will comply with City,

State and Federal regulations. Runoff during construction will be handled in conformance with the

City’s regulations and standards. A NPDES permit from the State of Washington Department of Ecology

for construction activities could be required as part of this project.

Nexus: MVMC Chapters 13.33 (Storm Water Drainage Utility), 15.16 (Grading, Excavation and Fill), and

Mount Vernon Engineering Standards.

3. CRITICAL AREAS

Impacts: The site contains a Category III wetland located in the northwest corner of the site. The

wetland is proposed to be filled, and mitigated with purchase of credits from an existing wetland bank in

Skagit County. The site is also considered an erosion hazard area, due to soils present, as well as a

SEPA Environmental Review Staff Report Page 3 of 6

SOS Storage, PLAN25-0449 and ENGR25-0286

seismic hazard area, due to the high potential for liquefaction. The site is also located within FEMA’s A21

Zone.

Mitigation Measures: Listed within the Applicant’s SEPA Checklist, detailed within the Preliminary

Drainage Report, shown on construction plans, the Wetland Mitigation Plan, and the mitigation

measures outlined within this MDNS. In addition, the applicant will comply with City, State, and Federal

regulations. Runoff during construction will be handled in conformance with the City’s regulations and

standards and Best Management Practices. Since issuance of the proposed MDNS, the applicant has

submitted a Wetland Mitigation Bank Use Plan to utilize off-site mitigation credits purchased from

Skagit Environmental Wetland Mitigation Bank.

The applicant will be required to comply with the City’s Critical Areas Code, the WA State Department of

Ecology’s rules/requirements and (potentially) the Federal Corps of Engineers rules/requirements.

Nexus: MVMC Chapter 15.06 (Environmental Policies), Chapter 15.40 (Critical Areas Code), RCW 90.48,

33 U.S. Code Chapter 26 (Federal Water Pollution Control Act)

4. TRAFFIC

Impacts: An estimated 205 new PM peak hour traffic trips will be generated from the proposed project.

There will also be construction related traffic that will occur as this site is being developed.

Mitigation Measures:  Compliance with the City’s MVMC provisions.

Nexus: MVMC Title 12, Chapter 14.10 (Concurrency Management), Chapter 3.40 (Impact Fees), 15.16

(Grading, Excavation and Fill), 16.16 (Design Standards for Non-arterial Streets) and Mount Vernon

Engineering Standards.

5. HISTORIC AND CULTURAL PRESERVATION

Impacts: The project is located in an area determined to have high risk of containing pre-contact

archaeological resources and a survey is highly advised for the site according to the Washington

Department of Archaeology and Historic Preservation’s (DAHP) predictive model. Accordingly, an

Archaeological Survey Report was prepared by ERCI for the proposed project. In addition, the site may

be of cultural and historic significance to local tribes. No protected cultural resources were identified

during fieldwork.

Mitigation Measures:  Compliance with the mitigation measures listed above in subsection D of this

document.

Nexus: The State Environmental Policy Act (SEPA) requires that all major actions sponsored, funded,

permitted, or approved by the state and/or local agencies undergo planning to ensure environmental

considerations such as impacts on historic and cultural resources are given due weight in decision

making. State implementing regulations are in WAC 197-11 and WAC 468-12 (WSDOT). For details on

SEPA procedures, see Chapter 400. Additionally, RCW 27.53: Archaeological Sites and Resources, and

RCW 27.44: Indian Graves and Records that strictly mandates the protection of human skeletal remains

and imposes a duty to notify law enforcement in the case of inadvertent discovery.

H. NOTES TO THE APPLICANT

Comments #1 and 2, below, will need to be addressed before future Building Permits are submitted to the City:

1. Geotechnical Engineering Report: this report describes the proposed project as consisting of “multiple

1- to 2- story metal-sided buildings supported by shallow conventional foundations and slab-on-grade

floors” (page 2 of report). Unfortunately, this description is not congruent with multiple other submittal

items stating that a four-story “u” shaped structure of approximately 32,000 sf for a hotel or other

similar use permitted in the C-2 zone is also proposed.

This report confirms the project site is considered an erosion hazard area and a seismic hazard exhibiting

a “high” liquefaction potential. This report also contains more than a dozen recommendations that

need to be followed based on the assumption that 1-2 story structures with shallow conventional

foundations and slab-on-grade floors will be constructed.

This report must be updated, or an addendum to this report must be prepared evaluating the proposed

4-story approximate 32,000 sf commercial structure. If all the conclusions and recommendations

existing within this report will be exactly the same for the structure type that was not included, an short

addendum stating this will satisfy this item.

2. Pilot Infiltration Testing Addendum: if the updated information requested for the Geotechnical

Engineering Report impacts this document the Applicant will need to have this addendum updated too.

3. Reminder: since the Applicant choose to withdraw their previously submitted Site Plan Permit there are

several aspects of this project (i.e. number of parking stalls, landscaping, setbacks, et cetera) that have

not been reviewed for consistency with the Mount Vernon Municipal Code.

ITEM: 7B

MEETING DATE: September 23, 2026

SUBJECT: Public Hearing and Approval of Ordinance - Data Center

Moratorium

PRESENTER: Kevin Rogerson

RECOMMENDED ACTION:

Approve Ordinance 3946

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY:

The City's current zoning and business licensing regulations do not address

data centers specifically nor generally in any meaningful comprehensive

fashion exposing the City to impacts associated with such facilities that may

be detrimental to the community. Impacts may include but are not limited

to significant water demand and usage, noise, water treatment, removal of

needed sewer treatment capacity and other environmental impacts, as well

as the catastrophic risk presented by flooding of the Skagit River, which is a

particular concern as much of the City lies within the river’s 100-year flood

plain.

Data centers present a unique use that could be considered under a range of

uses under the Mount Vernon Municipal Code and, given the concerns

addressed above, should be defined as a specific use with identification of

the zones where a data center may be permitted along with the process for

review and approval of applications under specific development regulations

though the City’s open and public planning process. The ordinance maintains

the status quo to allow sufficient time for the City to study and examine

data centers through its public participation process.

Consistent with state law, the ordinance shall require a public hearing to be

held within 60 days after the ordinance takes effect. The Ordinance further

orders that:

 The moratorium shall be effective initially for six months unless

terminated or renewed by the Council.

 This ordinance shall take effect immediately upon passage by the City

Council.

1. moratorium ordinance and attachment

Legal

ATTACHMENTS:

SUBJECT: Approval of Ordinances - Wastewater Rates and Sewer

Connection Fees

PRESENTER: Chris Phillips

RECOMMENDED ACTION:

Approve Ordinances 3947 and 3948

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY:

The City’s Wastewater collection rate method has not been updated since

2019. HDR, Inc., performed a Wastewater Rate and Connection Fee Study

to develop a utility rate and connection fee that generates the necessary

revenue to fund the utility’s operations and maintenance, Wastewater

Comprehensive & Capital Improvement Plans, and debt service payments.

The rate study analysis included:

- Revenue Requirement (financial plan)

- Cost of Service, identifies interclass subsidies

- Rate Design, development of rates considering the revenue requirement,

cost of service and policy

considerations.

- Connection Fee Analysis

City Council during committee discussions agreed to the following:

1. Wastewater Rate

- 2027: 7% rate increase

- 2028: 6.5% rate increase

- 2029 to 2032 – Average CPI of 5%

Note: City Staff will update City Council utilizing “Waterworth Utility

Software” during the 3rd Quarter of 2028 to determine if any adjustment is

required to Wastewater Utility Rates.

2. Wastewater Connection Fee

- 2027: $1,000 connection fee increase. New fee = $10,275.

- 2028: $1,000 connection fee increase. New fee = $11,275

Note: City Staff will update City Council utilizing “Waterworth Utility

Software” during the 3rd quarter of 2028 to determine if any adjustment is

required to Wastewater Connection Fees.

1. Ordinance 3947

2. Ordinance 3948

APPLICABLE DEPARTMENTAL REVIEW

ORDINANCE NO. 3947

AN ORDINANCE OF THE CITY OF MOUNT VERNON AMENDING SECTION 13.32.020 AND

13.32.040 OF THE MOUNT VERNON MUNICIPAL CODE SETTING RESIDENTIAL

WASTEWATER SERVICE RATES

WHEREAS,  pursuant to RCW 35.67.020, the City of Mount Vernon has full jurisdiction and

authority to maintain, operate, conduct, manage, regulate, control, and to fix, alter, regulate, and control

rates and charges for systems of sewerage; and

WHEREAS,  the City wishes to make a minor correction to the residential sewer rates previously

set forth in Ordinance 3801.

Now, Therefore, THE CITY COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF MOUNT VERNON, WASHINGTON,

DO HEREBY ORDAIN AS FOLLOWS:

SECTION 1.  That section 13.32.020 of the Mount Vernon Municipal Code is hereby amended, the

amended section to read as follows:

13.32.020. Residential rates.

A. The monthly sewer service rates for each residential unit are set at the following amounts:

1. Base Charge. The monthly base charge for each residential unit, effective January 1, 2027

,

shall be  $44.31. On January 1st of each year thereafter, the current monthly base charge for

each residential unit shall be revised by an inflationary adjustment based on the Consumer

Price Index (CPI) for the previous June. In  no event shall the  change in rates be less  than zero

percent in any given year. Unit prices and all other charges shall be to the nearest cent.

2. Consumption Charge. In  addition to the base charge, each residential unit shall pay a

consumption charge for each 100 cubic feet of water consumed in a month. The monthly

consumption charge, effective January 1, 2027, shall be  $4.12. On January 1st of each year

thereafter, the current monthly consumption charge for each residential  unit s shall  be r revised by

an inflationary adjustment based d on the Consumer Price  Index (CPI) for r the p previous June. In

no event shall the change in rates be less than zero percent in any given year. Unit prices and

all other charges shall be to the nearest cent.

B. It is the intent of this section to calculate r residential wastewater charges based on water usage that

enters the sewerage system, and not t on water used exclusively for irrigation or other outdoor

usage. Wastewater charges for residential units shall be calculated in the following manner:

1. For the six months from February through July, the wastewater consumption charge shall be

based on metered water consumption.

2. For the six months of August through January, the consumption charge for each such month

shall be based on a winter water average defined as the average water consumption of the

preceding billing months of November through February.

C. The city's finance director, or his or her designee, may use a calculated volume of the system-wide

average for the residential customer class, or prior water consumption records if those records are

more representative of expected usage, to calculate residential wastewater consumption charges

under the following circumstances:

1. When the water meter has been determined to be malfunctioning;

2. When customers have insufficient water consumption history to calculate the initial monthly

consumption charge or to establish a winter water average for the months of November

through February;

3. When water use in the period used to calculate average water consumption is not representative

of expected water use, such as rental property that is vacant between tenants or in the case of

seasonal customers; and

4. When customers are not served by a publicly owned water supply system.

D. The city's finance director shall establish administrative procedures for customers requesting a

review of their metered water consumption average. A reasonable fee may be charged to the

account to cover the administrative costs of processing the request if it is determined that the

customer's average is correct or is higher than the average currently under use.

SECTION 2. That section 13.32.040 of the Mount Vernon Municipal Code is hereby amended, the

amended section to read as follows:

13.32.040. Industrial rate schedule and other provisions.

A. An industrial unit is defined in MVMC § 13.04.120.

B. A sewer user that discharges industrial waste, as defined in MVMC § 13.04.130, but does

not meet the requirement for an industrial unit may be charged under the commercial rate

schedule.

C. Each industrial unit shall be charged an annual capacity charge for flow, BOD, and

suspended solids. Capacity charges shall be based on the permitted flow, BOD, and

suspended solids values indicated in the industrial unit's waste discharge permit. The annual

capacity charges may be paid over the entire year in one-twelfth increments per month.

D. In addition to the annual capacity charges, each industrial unit shall be charged a monthly

operating expense charge for flow, BOD, and suspended solids actually discharged into the

city's sewerage system. The monthly expense charges shall be based on measured values for

those parameters averaged over the industrial unit's monthly operating time in days.

E. Each industrial unit shall be charged a monthly administrative charge.

F. Rates applicable for capacity operating charges, expense charges, and administrative

charges shall be as follows:

1. Flow capacity charge: For each 1,000,000 gallons per day (mgd) or a proportional sum

for each fraction thereof of flow capacity stated in the industrial unit's waste discharge

permit, there shall be a monthly charge, effective January 1, 2027of $7,454.90On

, .

January 1st of each year thereafter, the current monthly charge shall be revised by an

inflationary adjustment based on the Consumer Price Index (CPI) for the previous

June. In no event shall the change in rates be less than zero percent in any given year.

Unit prices and all other charges shall be to the nearest cent.

2. BOD capacity charge: For each pound per day of BOD capacity stated in the industrial

unit's waste discharge permit there shall be a monthly charge, effective January 1,

2027of $6.47On January 1st of each year thereafter, for each pound per day of BOD

, .

capacity stated in the industrial unit's waste discharge permit the current monthly

charge shall be revised by an inflationary adjustment based on the Consumer Price

Index (CPI) for the previous June. In no event shall the change in rates be less than

zero percent in any given year. Unit prices and all other charges shall be to the nearest

cent.

3. Suspended solids capacity charge: For each pound per day of suspended solids capacity

stated in the industrial unit's waste discharge permit there shall be a monthly charge,

effective January 1, 2027, of $2.38. On January 1st of each year thereafter, for each

pound per day of suspended solids capacity stated in the industrial unit's waste

discharge permit the current monthly charge shall be revised by an inflationary

adjustment based on the Consumer Price Index (CPI) for the previous June. In no

event shall the change in rates be less than zero percent in any given year. Unit prices

and all other charges shall be to the nearest cent.

4. Flow expense charge: For each 1,000 gallons of flow discharged into the city's sewage

system in a one-month period a charge of $1.31 shall apply per 1,000 gallons per

month. On January 1st of each year thereafter, the current charge per 1,000 gallons per

month shall be revised by an inflationary adjustment based on the Consumer Price

Index (CPI) for the previous June. In no event shall the change in rates be less than zero

percent in any given year. Unit prices and all other charges shall be to the nearest cent.

5. BOD expense charge: For each pound of BOD discharged into the city's sewage

system in a one-month period a charge of $0.89 shall apply per pound per month. On

January 1st of each year thereafter, for each pound of BOD discharged into the city's

sewage system in a one-month period the current charge per pound per month shall be

revised by an inflationary adjustment based on the Consumer Price Index (CPI) for the

previous June. In no event shall the change in rates be less than zero percent in any

given year. Unit prices and all other charges shall be to the nearest cent.

6. Suspended solids expense charge: For each pound of suspended solids discharged into

the city's sewage system in a one-month period a charge of $.78 shall apply per

pound per month. On January 1st of each year thereafter, for each pound of suspended

solids discharged into the city's sewage system in a one-month period the current

charge per pound per month shall be revised by an inflationary adjustment based on the

Consumer Price Index (CPI) for the previous June. In no event shall the change in rates

be less than zero percent in any given year. Unit prices and all other charges shall be to

the nearest cent.

7. Administrative charge shall be $2,106.72 per month. On January 1st of each year

thereafter, the current administrative monthly charge shall be revised by an inflationary

adjustment based on the Consumer Price Index (CPI) for the previous June. In no event

shall the change in rates be less than zero percent in any given year. Unit prices and all

other charges shall be to the nearest cent.

G. It is unlawful for a waste discharge permit holder (permit holder) to discharge waste into the

Mount Vernon sewage system which is not in compliance with parameter values established

in the waste discharge permits issued by either the city or the Department of Ecology. The

public works director shall notify a permit holder of a violative discharge within seven

days of occurrence. The permit holder shall respond to the director in writing within

five days of notification describing the circumstances which caused the violative discharge.

If the director determines that a violative discharge was not of an accidental nature, a

penalty charge shall be assessed, except as provided in subsection J of this section.

1. For industrial units, the penalty charge shall be as follows:

a. For purposes of calculating the penalty charge, the director shall use the average

discharge values of the three highest consecutive operating days within a 30-day

period (month) for determining the excess discharge. Nonoperating days indicated

by abnormally low loads will not be included in the average. The excess discharge

shall be the average of the three highest consecutive operating days less the permit

value. The penalty charge for the month shall be the excess discharge times the

appropriate unit charge. The discharge value shall be based on a 24-hour composite

sample. Unless modified by council action, the penalty charge shall be based on

the following unit charges:

b. Unit charges shall be as follows:

i. Flow. For each 1,000 gallons of flow in excess of the permit value, the

discharger shall pay $20.61. On January 1st of each year thereafter, the

current charge for each 1,000 gallons of flow in excess of the permit value

shall be revised by an inflationary adjustment based on the Consumer Price

Index (CPI) for the previous June. In no event shall the change in rates be less

than zero percent in any given year. Unit prices and all other charges shall be

to the nearest cent.

ii. BOD. For each pound of BOD in excess of the permit value, the discharger

shall pay $16.68. On January 1st of each year thereafter, the current charge

for each pound of BOD in excess of the permit value shall be revised by an

inflationary adjustment based on the Consumer Price Index (CPI) for the

previous June. In no event shall the change in rates be less than zero percent

in any given year. Unit prices and all other charges shall be to the nearest

cent.

iii. SS. For each pound of suspended solids in excess of the permit value, the

discharger shall pay $6.81. On January 1st of each year thereafter, the current

charge for each pound of suspended solids in excess of the permit value shall

be revised by an inflationary adjustment based on the Consumer Price Index

(CPI) for the previous June. In no event shall the change in rates be less than

zero percent in any given year. Unit prices and all other charges shall be to

the nearest cent.

Sample calculation of penalty charge:

Waste discharge permit value:

Flow = 0.270 mgd

BOD = 1,000 ppd

SS = 550 ppd

Highest three consecutive operating days in month:

Flow: June 9 0.250 mgd

June 10 0.270 mgd

June 11 0.275 mgd

Average = (0.250 + 0.270 + 0.275)/3 = 0.265 m mgd

0.265 is less than 0.270 so there is no flow penalty charge.

BOD: June 11 1,100 pounds

June 12 no operation

June 13 no operation

June 14 1,200 pounds

June 15 1,500 pounds

Average = (1,100 + 1,200 + 1,500)/3 = 1,266.67 p ppd

BOD penalty charge = (1,266.67 – – 1,000) ($11.93) = $3,181.37

SS: June 15 600 pounds

June 16 650 p pounds

June 17 450 p pounds

Average = (600 + 650 + 450)/3 = 566.67 p pounds

SS penalty charge = (566.67 – 550) ($4.87) = $81.18

ORDINANCE NO. 3948

AN ORDINANCE OF THE CITY OF MOUNT VERNON, WASHINGTON, AMENDING THE

SEWER CONNECTION FEE CONTAINED WITHIN CHAPTER 13.12.040 AND 13.12.090 TO

INCREASE THE CONNECTION FEE WHEN BUILDING PERMITS ARE ISSUED

WHEREAS, the proposed amendments are not subject to the SEPA process or RCW

36.70A.106; and

WHEREASstate law has granted authority to Cities to require connection fees

,

for the hook up of property owners seeking to connect to the City’s sewerage system as a

condition to granting the right to so connect in addition to the cost of such connection;

and

WHEREASthe City Council of the City of Mount Vernon finds that the

,

wastewater connection charges enacted in April 1990 pursuant to Ordinance No. 2386

,

Ordinance No. 2392 and Ordinance No. 3414 need to be updated to ensure that new

wastewater connections are assessed a fair and equitable share of the cost of the

wastewater utility system; and

WHEREAS, the City has completed a Wastewater Connection Charge Study

dated August 2026 and prepared by HDR Engineering, Inc. with the objective to establish

a cost-based connection charge that ensures equity between existing and new customers

of the wastewater utility system and directs this study to be entered into the record; and

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE CITY COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF

MOUNT VERNON, WASHINGTON, AS FOLLOWS:

SECTION ONE.  The City Council does hereby adopt the above listed recitals as set forth fully herein.

SECTION TWOSection 13.12.040Permit – – Wastewater connection charge required, of the Mount

.  ,

Vernon Municipal Code is hereby repealed and reenacted with the new section to read as follows:

13.12.040 Permit – – Wastewater connection charge required.

For each connection permit there shall be paid by the applicant a wastewater connection charge as

follows:

A. Residential Occupancies. Except as provided in subsections DE and G of this section, the

,

wastewater connection charge for residential construction shall be $5,360 per equivalent residential unit

(ERU).

B. Except as provided in subsection G, duduplex connections shall pay 75 percent of the residential

occupancy rate per unit, or $4,020 per unit.

C. Commercial Occupancies. Except as provided in subsection E and G of this section, the wastewater

connection charge for nonresidential construction shall be $255.00 per fixture unit (as defined pursuant

to the Uniform Plumbing Code). At the discretion of the public works director, the peak flow water

consumption may be used to determine the wastewater connection charge when the number of fixture

units cannot be determined, such as with some industrial uses.

D. Except as provided in subsection G, Mixed Occupancies. Mixed residential and nonresidential uses

shall be charged at both residential and nonresidential rates in proportion to the use, as necessary.

Ordinance 3948

Page 1 of 3

E. Multifamily Construction. Except as provided in subsection G, apapplicants for sewer connection

permits for multiple-family construction may request that the wastewater connection charge be

calculated at the rate of $153.00 per fixture unit. It shall be the burden of the applicant to clearly

demonstrate the number of fixture units to be included in the construction, and that it is not likely that

additional fixture units will be added after payment of the connection charges. Otherwise, multifamily

construction will be charged 50 percent of the residential occupancy connection charge per unit, or

$2,680 per unit.

F. Developed Properties. Applicants for sewer connection permits for any previously developed property

within city limits that has an on-site sewage system as of February 1, 2005, shall be exempt from the

payment of a wastewater connection charge. For the purposes of this subsection, a "previously

developed property" means a parcel of real property on which a building or other structure has been

erected prior to February 1, 2005, and which building or structure had received approval for occupancy

by the applicable unit of local government authorized to approve such occupancy.

G. Fee Increase. Beginning on January 1, 2027 the connection charge shall be as indicated in the table

below:

Sewer Connection Fee Category Effective January 1, 2027

Residential $10,275

Duplex (75% of Residential) $7,705

Commercial (per fixture unit) $486

Multi-family (50% of Residential) $5,136

Multi-family (per fixture unit option) $294

st

SECTION THREE.  Section 13.12.090, Connection charge annual escalation, of the Mount Vernon

Municipal Code is hereby repealed and reenacted with the new section to read as follows:

13.12.090 Connection charge annual escalation.

Each year beginning January 1, 2029, the wastewater connection charge will be adjusted by the

Engineering News Record – – Construction Cost Index for the Seattle area (ENR-CCI), and the Existing

System and Future Capital Costs related to Treatment and Collection as determined by the Public Works

Director.

SECTION FOUR. Severability. If any section, subsection, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of

this ordinance is declared unconstitutional or invalid for any reason, such decision shall not affect the

validity of the remaining parts of this ordinance.
